\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\varnothing\varnothing}[[m,n]].

(4.38)

Tuning
\mu=q_{4}
, this reduces to the PT3 vertex in
(
Kimura-Noshita-PT3
, Section 3.4.2)
. Note that if we take
\mu=q_{3}
, we instead obtain the PT3 vertex using the D6
{}_{\bar{3}}
-branes.

Generic case

The above example is an example where the minimal solid partition is restricted to a three-dimensional subspace and the boundary plane partitions are two-dimensional Young diagrams. For such cases, the PT4 vertex will be a one parameter (
\mu
) deformation of the PT3 vertex and tuning the parameter
\mu
reproduces it.

Let us consider an example where the boundary plane partitions are purely three-dimensional:

\displaystyle\pi_{1}=1+q_{2}+q_{4},\quad\pi_{2}=1+q_{1}+q_{3}+q_{4},\quad\pi_{3}=\pi_{4}=\varnothing

(4.39)

We then obtain the hollow structure

\displaystyle\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{PT4Figures/PT4_twoleg_2.pdf}}}}}}}$}

(4.40)

Each slice looks like the hollow structure of the PT3 vertex.

The framing node contribution is given as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\pi_{3},\pi_{4}}^{\text{D}8\text{--}\text{D}2\text{--}\text{D}0}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\phi_{I})=\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}(b-\phi_{I})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1}-2\epsilon_{3})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}+2\epsilon_{4}+\epsilon_{3})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{4})}{\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{12}-\phi_{I})\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{4}-\phi_{I})\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{2}-\phi_{I})}

(4.41)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{3}-2\epsilon_{4})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}+2\epsilon_{3}+\epsilon_{4})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-2\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{4})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-2\epsilon_{12})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-2\epsilon_{3})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{13})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-2\epsilon_{4})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{124})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{34})}.

Choosing the reference vector
\phi=\tilde{\eta}_{0}
, at level one, the poles picked up are

\displaystyle\phi_{1}=\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2},\quad\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{2},\quad\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{4}.

(4.42)

The first two-poles are actually the boxes at level one for the PT3 vertex
\mathcal{Z}^{\text{PT}\text{--}\text{JK}}_{\bar{4};\lambda\mu\nu}[\mathfrak{q},q_{1,2,3,4}]
with boundary conditions
\lambda=\hskip 0.0pt\vbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt,\,\mu=\hskip 0.0pt\vbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt,\,\nu=\varnothing
, and the last pole is the box at level one for the case
\lambda=\hskip 0.0pt\vbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt,\,\mu=\hskip 0.0pt\vbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt\hbox to5.4pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.32pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.07997pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt,\,\nu=\varnothing
.

Studying higher levels, the PT4 configurations can be understood as slices of PT3 configurations. Additionally, each PT3 slices need to obeying the melting rule Cond.
4.1
in the fourth direction. In other words, the boxes need to be also supported from the positive direction of the 4-axis.

If instead one of the two-legs point the fourth direction, the illustration will look like

\displaystyle\cdots\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{PT4Figures/PT4_twoleg_3.pdf}}}}}}}$}

(4.43)

where
\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}=\varnothing
and
\pi_{3}=1+q_{2}+q_{4},\pi_{4}=1+q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3}
. Instead of coloring the boxes of
\pi_{4}
in orange, we colored it with light-blue as other boxes in the boundary conditions. The second slice from the right has the coordinate
0
and the right-most one has
\epsilon_{4}
. Note again that the plane partition
\pi_{4}
extends semi-infinitely to the left.

In this description, each slice no more looks like a PT3 configuration anymore. Even so, the rules to stack boxes are the same as Cond.
4.1
and one can list down possible PT4 configurations easily. For example, one can stack boxes at
\epsilon_{4}+\epsilon_{3}
,
\epsilon_{2}
,
-\epsilon_{4}+\epsilon_{1}
at the first level, because all these boxes do not need to be supported from the positive direction.

4.3
Three-legs

When there are three-legs, similar to the PT3 vertex, the contour integrand contains maximally second-order poles. For an example, we only consider the case

\displaystyle\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}=\pi_{3}=1,\quad\pi_{4}=\varnothing.

(4.44)

Other examples when the legs are purely plane partitions, but not Young diagrams, can be discussed in a similar way but we will focus only on this example.

The hollow structure is

\displaystyle\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{PT4Figures/PT4_threeleg_1.pdf}}}}}}}$}

(4.45)

For this example, the PT4 configurations are the same as the PT3 configurations and the partition function is a one-parameter deformation of the PT3 computation.

Let us derive the PT4 vertex for low levels using the JK-residue formalism. The discussion here is a 4-fold version of the one given in
(
Kimura-Noshita-PT3
, section 3.4.3)
. We reproduce the discussion again here.

The flavor node contribution is

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\hbox to4.89pt{\vbox to4.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}{{}}{}
{}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\hbox to4.89pt{\vbox to4.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}{{}}{}
{}{}

Note that tuning
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{4}
, it reduces to the framing node contribution coming from the D6-brane (see (

)).

Level two

Let us next consider the second level. Again, choosing
\eta=(-1,\ldots,-1)
, the poles picked up come from

\displaystyle-\phi_{I}+\mathfrak{a}=0,\quad\phi_{I}-\phi_{J}=-\epsilon_{1,2,3,4}.

(4.51)

Using the Weyl invariance, let us focus on the following cases

\displaystyle(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}),\quad(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3,4}).

(4.52)

Let us check which poles remain non-zero. We start first from the pole
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})
. In usual instanton computations, contributions such as this disappear because of the numerator
\prod_{I\neq J}\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\phi_{J})
. One would expect that a similar cancellation happens here but this is not the case.
4
4

4

This phenomenon occurs also in the PT3 computation (see
(
Kimura-Noshita-PT3
, section 3.4.3)
).

(4.53)

where
f_{0}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
is a function with no zeros nor poles at
\phi_{1}=\phi_{2}=\mathfrak{a}
. The residue is then computed as

\displaystyle=\left.\partial_{\phi_{1}}\left(\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})^{2}f_{0}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}}\right)\right|_{\phi_{1}=\mathfrak{a}}

(4.54)

\displaystyle=\partial_{\phi_{1}}f_{0}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})|_{\phi_{1}=\mathfrak{a}}-\frac{f_{0}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})\operatorname{ch}(\mathfrak{a}-\phi_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{2}\simeq\mathfrak{a}}-\frac{2f_{0}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})}

where we used
\operatorname{sh}(x)^{\prime}=\operatorname{ch}(x)/2
and
\operatorname{ch}(0)=2
. Obviously, we still have a pole at
\phi_{2}=\mathfrak{a}
and thus the residue is non-zero.

(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3})
:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{f_{1}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{1}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}}\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}+\epsilon_{1})}=\frac{f_{2}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{1}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}}\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}+\epsilon_{2})}=\frac{f_{3}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{1}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}}\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{3})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}+\epsilon_{3})}

where
f_{1,2,3}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
are functions with no zeros and poles at
\phi_{2}=\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3}
, respectively. Using the triality, let us focus on the
\epsilon_{3}
case. Looking at the integrand, since there is
\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{3})
, one might expect that the residue at
\phi_{2}=\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{3}
vanishes. Again, this is not the case because when evaluating the residue at
\phi_{1}=\mathfrak{a}
, derivatives of the integrand appears:

\displaystyle\underset{\phi_{1}=\mathfrak{a}}{\Res}\prod_{I=1}^{2}\mathcal{Z}_{\hbox to4.89pt{\vbox to4.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

\displaystyle=\left.\partial_{\phi_{1}}\left(f_{3}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{3})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1}+\epsilon_{3})}\right)\right|_{\phi_{1}=\mathfrak{a}}

(4.56)

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{3}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{3})}+\partial_{\phi_{1}}f_{3}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2}).

We still have a single pole appearing in the denominator and thus this pole gives a non-zero residue.

(4.57)

where
f_{4}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
is a function with no zeros and poles. The residue at

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0)}+2\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{i})}\right),

(4.60)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}}{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{1})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(-\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{1}\right)}{2\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{2})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{2}\right)}{2\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(-\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+2\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{3}\right)}{2\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)}.

The overall factor is the Weyl group factor
1/2
. For the configurations coming from the poles
(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3})
, we have two situations in taking the residue
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{i}),\,(\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{i},\mathfrak{a})
and the Weyl group factor is canceled out by such multiplicity.

(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}),(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3})

\phi_{3}

(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})

4.53
) and (
4.54

The poles
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{31,32,34}
all cancel with the numerator of
\mathcal{Z}^{\text{D}0\text{--}\text{D}0}(\phi_{1},\phi_{3})|_{\phi_{1}=\mathfrak{a}}
, while the pole
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}-2\epsilon_{3}
is non-zero. Moreover, the pole at
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}
also gives non-zero contribution.

Summarizing, for the level three, the non-zero contributions come from the poles

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{i}),\quad(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{i},\mathfrak{a}-2\epsilon_{i}),\quad i=1,2,3.

{}{}{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 4.48 L 4.48 4.48 L 4.48 0 Z M 4.48 4.48 M 0 4.48 L 1.73 6.21 L 6.21 6.21 L 4.48 4.48 M 4.48 0 L 6.21 1.73 L 6.21 6.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\varnothing}^{\text{D}8\text{--}\text{D}2\text{--}\text{D}0}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\phi_{I})\prod_{I<J}\mathcal{Z}^{\text{D}0\text{--}\text{D}0}(\phi_{I},\phi_{J})

(4.66)

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\bar{4};\,\hbox to4.45pt{\vbox to4.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}} {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 5.6 L 5.6 5.6 L 5.6 0 Z M 5.6 5.6}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\hbox to4.45pt{\vbox to4.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}} {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 5.6 L 5.6 5.6 L 5.6 0 Z M 5.6 5.6}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\hbox to4.45pt{\vbox to4.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}} {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 5.6 L 5.6 5.6 L 5.6 0 Z M 5.6 5.6}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\text{PT}\text{--}\text{JK}}[3]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\left(3\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0,-\epsilon_{i})}+6\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{i},-2\epsilon_{i})}\right),

(4.67)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0,-\epsilon_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(-\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{1}\right)}{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{1}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0,-\epsilon_{2})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{2}\right)}{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{2}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0,-\epsilon_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(-\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+2\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}+2\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{3}\right)}{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{1},-2\epsilon_{1})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(-2\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(3\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+2\epsilon_{1}\right)}{\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{1}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(3\epsilon_{1}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{2},-2\epsilon_{2})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}-2\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+3\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+2\epsilon_{2}\right)}{\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{2}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(3\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{3},-2\epsilon_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}\left(-\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}+2\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+3\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\mathfrak{b}+2\epsilon_{3}\right)}{\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(2\epsilon_{3}\right)^{2}\operatorname{sh}\left(3\epsilon_{3}\right)\operatorname{sh}\left(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{3}\right)}.

Generic level
k\geq 2

Similar to the PT3 case, two classes of poles only remain:

\displaystyle(0,-\epsilon_{i},\ldots,-(k-1)\epsilon_{i})

(4.68)

and

\displaystyle(0,0,-\epsilon_{1},\ldots,-n_{1}\epsilon_{1},-\epsilon_{2},\ldots,-n_{2}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3},\ldots,-n_{3}\epsilon_{3}),\quad n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}=k-2.

(4.69)

We thus have
\binom{k}{2}+3
possible configurations. The JK-residue schematically takes the form as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\text{PT}\text{--}\text{JK}}_{\bar{4};\,\hbox to4.45pt{\vbox to4.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}} {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 5.6 L 5.6 5.6 L 5.6 0 Z M 5.6 5.6}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\hbox to4.45pt{\vbox to4.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}} {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 5.6 L 5.6 5.6 L 5.6 0 Z M 5.6 5.6}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\hbox to4.45pt{\vbox to4.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}} {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 5.6 L 5.6 5.6 L 5.6 0 Z M 5.6 5.6}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}[k]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k!}\left(k!\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{i},\ldots,-(k-1)\epsilon_{i})}\right.

(4.70)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{k!}{2!}\sum_{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}=k-2}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0,-\epsilon_{1},\ldots,-n_{1}\epsilon_{1},-\epsilon_{2},\ldots,-n_{2}\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3},\ldots,-n_{3}\epsilon_{3})}\right),

where each term is understood as the JK-residue of the contour integrand without the Weyl group factor for each configuration.

4.4
Four-legs

Let us move on to the nontrivial example where there are all four legs. As an example, we only discuss the example:

\displaystyle\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}=\pi_{3}=\pi_{4}=1.

(4.71)

After extending the minimal solid partition and removing the boxes in the positive quadrant, we obtain

\displaystyle\cdots\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{PT4Figures/PT4_fourleg_1.pdf}}}}}}}$}

(4.72)

Note that there are boxes at the origin extending semi-infinitely from the right to the left.

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}(-\phi_{I}+\mathfrak{b})\prod_{a\in\underline{\textbf{4}}}\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{a})^{2}}{\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{a}-\phi_{I})^{3}\prod_{A\in\underline{\textbf{6}}}\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{I}-\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{A})}

(4.73)

Choosing the reference vector
\eta=\tilde{\eta}_{0}
picks the pole

\displaystyle-\phi_{1}+\mathfrak{a}=0

(4.74)

and it is a third-order pole. The existence of this third-order pole is a new perspective of the PT4 counting. The JK-residue is evaluated as

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{ch}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}g_{0}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})}{4\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})^{3}}+\frac{g_{0}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})}{4\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})}-\frac{\operatorname{ch}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})g^{(1,0)}_{0}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}}+\frac{g_{0}^{(2,0)}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})}{2\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})}

denotes the
m
(
n
) derivative of
\phi_{1}

(\phi_{2})
, respectively. Obviously, there is a pole at
\phi_{2}=\mathfrak{a}
and thus, we still can take the residue at
\phi_{2}=\mathfrak{a}
. Similar to the three-legs case, we have the PT4 configuration coming from
\phi_{1}=\phi_{2}=\mathfrak{a}
.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\hbox to4.89pt{\vbox to4.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 0 0 L 0 4.48 L 4.48 4.48 L 4.48 0 Z M 4.48 4.48 M 0 4.48 L 1.73 6.21 L 6.21 6.21 L 4.48 4.48 M 4.48 0 L 6.21 1.73 L 6.21 6.21}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\text{D}8\text{--}\text{D}2\text{--}\text{D}0}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\phi_{I})\mathcal{Z}^{\text{D}0\text{--}\text{D}0}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{ch}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1})^{2}g_{1}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})}{4\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1})}-\frac{1}{8}g_{1}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1})

(4.83)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\text{ch}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1})g_{1}^{(1,0)}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1})g^{(2,0)}_{1}(\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2})

which gives a single pole. Thus, the pole
\phi_{1}=\mathfrak{a},\phi_{2}=\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1}
gives non-zero JK-residue contribution. Summarizing, the poles giving the non-zero JK-residues are

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}),\,\,(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3,4}).

(4.84)

Explicitly, the JK-residues are given as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{ch}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{ch}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\sum_{a\in\underline{\textbf{4}}}\frac{\operatorname{ch}(\epsilon_{a})}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{a})}

(4.85)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}\left(\frac{\operatorname{ch}(\epsilon_{12})^{2}}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{12})^{2}}+\frac{\operatorname{ch}(\epsilon_{13})^{2}}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{13})^{2}}+\frac{\operatorname{ch}(\epsilon_{23})^{2}}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{23})^{2}}\right)+\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})^{2}\sum_{A\in\underline{\textbf{6}}}\prod_{a\in A}\frac{\operatorname{ch}(\epsilon_{a})}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{a})},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{4})}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(2\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{12})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{32})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{13})},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{2})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{2})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{2})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{4})}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{2})\operatorname{sh}(2\epsilon_{2})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{12})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{32})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{13})},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{3})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{3})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{2})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{4})}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{3})\operatorname{sh}(2\epsilon_{3})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{12})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{32})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{13})},

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{4})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{4})\operatorname{sh}(-\epsilon_{4}+\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{4})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{3}-\epsilon_{4})}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{4})\operatorname{sh}(2\epsilon_{4})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{12})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{32})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{13})}

and the PT4 partition function at second level is

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\text{PT}\text{--}\text{JK}}_{\hbox to4.89pt{\vbox to4.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0)}+2\sum_{a\in\underline{\textbf{4}}}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,-\epsilon_{a})}\right).

(4.86)

Level three

Let us assume
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})
first and consider the pole at
\phi_{3}
. JK-residue pickes the poles at

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{a}+\phi_{3}=0,\quad\phi_{I}-\phi_{3}=\epsilon_{1,2,3,4}.

(4.87)

Let us first study the pole coming from
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}
. The integrand schematically takes the form as

\displaystyle\prod_{I=1}^{3}\mathcal{Z}_{\hbox to4.89pt{\vbox to4.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

(4.88)

Performing the residue at
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a})
gives

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,\hbox to4.89pt{\vbox to4.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\text{D}8\text{--}\text{D}2\text{--}\text{D}0}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\phi_{I})\prod_{I<J}\mathcal{Z}^{\text{D}0\text{--}\text{D}0}(\phi_{I},\phi_{J})

(4.89)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3\text{ch}\left(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}\right){}^{2}h_{0}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)}{2\text{sh}\left(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}\right)}+\text{ch}\left(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}\right)h_{0}{}^{(0,1,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)+\text{ch}\left(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}\right)h_{0}{}^{(1,0,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{4}\text{sh}\left(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}\right)h_{0}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\text{sh}\left(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}\right)h_{0}{}^{(0,2,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)-2\text{sh}\left(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}\right)h_{0}{}^{(1,1,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\text{sh}\left(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}\right)h_{0}{}^{(2,0,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)

where
h_{0}^{(a,b,c)}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})
denotes the
a,b,c
derivatives of the first, second, third variables, respectively. We still have a single pole here and the residue is still non-zero at
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}
:

\displaystyle(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}).

(4.90)

This is a new phenomenon not appearing in the PT3 counting or the three-legs examples discussed before and we can place three boxes at the same position. Actually this phenomenon is what one would have expected because we had a third order pole at the origin. For this case, the non-zero JK-residue is computed as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\mathcal{Z}_{(0,0,0)}=\left(\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\mathfrak{b}-\mathfrak{a})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{12})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{13})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{23})}{\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{2})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{3})\operatorname{sh}(\epsilon_{123})}\right)^{3}.

(4.91)

Let us consider the other poles with
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3,4}
. Using the quadrality symmetry, it is enough to consider the case when
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}-\phi_{1}
:

\displaystyle\prod_{I=1}^{3}\mathcal{Z}_{\hbox to4.89pt{\vbox to4.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}}{}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2})^{2}\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{3}-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1})^{2}h_{1}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})}{\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{1}-\mathfrak{a})^{3}\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{2}-\mathfrak{a})^{3}\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{3}-\phi_{1}+\epsilon_{1})\operatorname{sh}(\phi_{3}-\phi_{2}+\epsilon_{1})}.

(4.92)

Taking the residue gives

(4.93)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{h_{1}{}^{(0,1,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)\text{ch}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1}+\phi_{3}\right)}{2\text{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1}+\phi_{3}\right)}-\frac{h_{1}{}^{(1,0,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)\text{ch}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1}+\phi_{3}\right)}{2\text{sh}\left(-\mathfrak{a}+\epsilon_{1}+\phi_{3}\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}h_{1}{}^{(0,2,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)-2h_{1}{}^{(1,1,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)+\frac{1}{2}h_{1}{}^{(2,0,0)}\left(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\phi_{3}\right)

and we have a single pole at
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1}
. Thus, the following poles give non-zero JK-residues

\displaystyle(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3,4}).

(4.94)

The residues are complicated so we omit the explicit expression.

Let us next assume
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1,2,3,4})
. Using the quadrality symmetry, it is enough to consider
(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{1})
. JK-residue picks the poles at

\displaystyle\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a},\quad\phi_{3}=\phi_{2}-\epsilon_{1,2,3,4}.

(4.95)

The pole coming from
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}
actually reduces to the previous computation, so let us focus on
\phi_{3}=\phi_{2}-\epsilon_{1,2,3,4}=\mathfrak{a}-2\epsilon_{1},\mathfrak{a}-\epsilon_{12,13,14}
. Focusing at the
\phi_{3}=\mathfrak{a}-2\epsilon_{1}
, the integrand schematically takes the form as
